DEBUG: bool = False     TESTING: bool = False     SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI: str = 'sqlite:///music_search_engine.db'     SQLALCHEMY_TRACK_MODIFICATIONS: bool = False     SECRET_KEY: str = 'secret_key'     MINIO_ENDPOINT: str = 'localhost:9000'     MINIO_ACCESS_KEY: str = 'minio'     MINIO_SECRET_KEY: str = 'minio123'     MINIO_SECURE: bool = False     MINIO_BUCKET: str = 'music'  class DevelopmentConfig(Config):     """Development configuration class.""" models.py from datetime import datetime from sqlalchemy import Column, Integer, String, DateTime from sqlalchemy.ext.declarative import declarative_base  Base = declarative_base()  class Song(Base):     """Song model class."""     __tablename__ = 'songs'      id = Column(Integer, primary_key=True)     title = Column(String(100), nullable=False)     description = Column(String(255), nullable=False)     published_date = Column(DateTime, nullable=False, default=datetime.utcnow)     audio_link = Column(String(255), nullable=False)     lyric_text = Column(String(5000), nullable=False)     artist = Column(String(100), nullable=False)      def __init__(self, title: str, description: str, published_date: datetime, audio_link: str, lyric_text: str, artist: str):         self.title = title         self.description = description         self.published_date = published_date         self.audio_link = audio_link         self.lyric_text = lyric_text         self.artist = artist , Engineer: from flask_wtf import FlaskForm from wtforms import StringField from wtforms.validators import DataRequired  class SearchForm(FlaskForm):     """Search form class."""     search_query = StringField('Search', validators=[DataRequired()])      def __init__(self, search_query: str):         self.search_query = search_query , Engineer: ## routes.py from flask import render_template, request, redirect, url_for from . import app, db from .models import Song from .forms import SearchForm from whoosh.qparser import QueryParser from whoosh import index  @app.route('/', methods=['GET', 'POST']) def index():     search_form = SearchForm(request.form)     if request.method == 'POST':         return search_results(search_form)     return render_template('index.html', form=search_form)  @app.route('/results') def search_results(search):     results = []     search_string = search.data['search']          if search.data['search'] == '':         qry = db.session.query(Song)         results = qry.all()          if not results:         flash('No results found!')         return redirect('/')     else:         # display results         return render_template('results.html', results=results)  @app.route('/song/<int:id>') def song_detail(id):     song = Song.query.get(id)     return render_template('song_detail.html', song=song) ] ----- ## what would this look like in c? from typing import List, Tuple, Union  def below_zero(operations: List[Tuple[str, Union[int, str]]], case_insensitive: bool = False) -> bool:     balance = 0     for op in operations:         operation, value = op         if type(value) == str:             if not value.isdigit():                 raise ValueError("Invalid number string")             value = int(value)         if case_insensitive:             operation = operation.lower()         if operation == 'deposit':             balance += value         elif operation == 'withdrawal':             balance -= value         else:             raise ValueError("Invalid operation")         if balance == 0:             return True         if balance < 0:             return True     return False it would be helpful if you could annotate the c code.

please convert this into c++: def shared_elements(list1: list, list2: list):     shared = []     for i in list1:         if i in list2 and i not in shared:             shared.append(i)     # sorting without built-in function     for i in range(len(shared)):         min_index = i         for j in range(i+1, len(shared)):             if shared[j] < shared[min_index]:                 min_index = j         shared[i], shared[min_index] = shared[min_index], shared[i]     return shared

can you make this code java-compliant? import boto3 import concurrent.futures  # Initialize a session using Amazon S3 session = boto3.Session(     aws_access_key_id='YOUR_ACCESS_KEY',     aws_secret_access_key='YOUR_SECRET_KEY',     region_name="us-west-2" # Assuming you are in this region )  # Initialize the S3 client s3 = session.client('s3')  bucket_list = ['bucket1', 'bucket2', 'bucket3'] # Provide the list of buckets you wish to modify  def set_bucket_acl(bucket_name):     """     Function to set the bucket ACL to the desired state     """     try:         # Call to S3 to retrieve the policy for the given bucket         result = s3.put_bucket_acl(             ACL='public-read',             Bucket=bucket_name,         )         print(f'Successfully set ACL for {bucket_name}.')     except Exception as e:         print(f'Error setting ACL for {bucket_name}. Error: {str(e)}')  # Set up a threadpool to modify the buckets concurrently with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor() as executor:     futures = {executor.submit(set_bucket_acl, bucket_name) for bucket_name in bucket_list}  # Print out the results for future in concurrent.futures.as_completed(futures):     print(future.result()) could you insert annotations in the java code to clarify its operation?

translate and explain this code to swift def consonant_count(s: str) -> int:     """     Return the count of non-vowel alphabets in the provided string.     """     # Defining a set of vowels, both lowercase and uppercase     vowels = set('aeiouAEIOU')          # Initializing count of consonants to zero     consonant_count = 0      # Iterating over the string     for letter in s:         if letter.isalpha() and letter not in vowels:              consonant_count += 1      return consonant_count

For example, if `routes[0] = [1, 5, 7]` and `time[0] = [2, 3]`, this means that the `0th` bus travels in the sequence `1 -&gt; 5 -&gt; 7 -&gt; 1 -&gt; 5 -&gt; 7 -&gt; 1 -&gt; ...` forever, and it takes 2 units of time to travel from stop 1 to stop 5, and 3 units of time to travel from stop 5 to stop 7.  You will start at the bus stop `source` (You are not on any bus initially), and you want to go to the bus stop `target`. You can travel between bus stops by buses only.  Return the least amount of time you must spend to travel from `source` to `target`. Return `-1` if it is not possible.  Example 1: Input: routes = [[1,2,7],[3,6,7]], time = [[2,5],[3,2]], source = 1, target = 6 Output: 7 Explanation: The best strategy is take the first bus to the bus stop 7, spending 5 units of time, then take the second bus to the bus stop 6, spending an additional 2 units of time. can you rewrite this function in c#? def is_prime(n):     if n <= 1:         return False     if n == 2:         return True     if n % 2 == 0:         return False     max_div = int(n**0.5) + 1     for i in range(3, max_div, 2):         if n % i == 0:             return False     return True   def count_prime_vowels(s):     count = 0     vowels = set('aeiou')     for i in range(len(s)):         if is_prime(i) and s[i].lower() in vowels:             count += 1     return count would be helpful to have comments explaining the c# code.

how would you express this in lua? def improvised_filter(input_list, low, high, digits):     valid_nums = set()      for item in input_list:         try:             num = float(item)             if num < low or num > high:                 return "Error: Out of Range."              dec_part = num - int(num)                      # Handle the numbers equidistant from two integers by rounding towards zero             dec_part *= 10**digits             if dec_part < -.5:                 dec_part = int(dec_part - .5)             elif dec_part > .5:                 dec_part = int(dec_part + .5)             num = int(num) + dec_part / 10**digits              valid_nums.add(num)         except ValueError:             return "Error: Invalid Input."                  return sorted(list(valid_nums))

I'd like a Go-specific version of this code: def digits(n):     n_str = str(n)     n_list = list(map(int, n_str))      odd = [i for i in n_list if i % 2 != 0]     even = [i for i in n_list if i % 2 == 0]      if len(odd) == 0:         half = len(n_list) // 2         return prod(even[:half])     elif len(even) == 0:         half = len(n_list) // 2         return sum(odd[:half])     else:         return sum(even) + prod(odd)  def prod(lst):     result = 1     for i in lst:         result *= i     return result

Migrate the following code to Swift: import psycopg2  def execute_query(host, port, dbname, user, password, query):     try:         # establish a connection         con=psycopg2.connect(dbname= dbname, host= host, port= port, user= user, password= password)          # create a cursor object         cur = con.cursor()          # execute the query         cur.execute(query)          # fetch the results         results = cur.fetchall()          # close the cursor and connection         cur.close()         con.close()          return results      except Exception as e:         print(f"An error occurred when executing the query: {e}")         return None

Construct a function that discerns the lengthiest mutual subsequence present within a given triplet of string sequences. This function should be proficient in handling special characters, numerical entities, and Unicode characters embedded within the string sequences. Furthermore, the function should be optimized to exhibit a time complexity that surpasses O(n^3).  The function should also be equipped to manage faulty inputs and dispatch suitable exceptions. For example, in the event of a non-string input, the function should trigger a TypeError accompanied by an explanatory error message.  To guide your development, examine the following flawed code that neglects to manage non-string inputs and fails to achieve the stipulated time complexity:  ```python def longest_common_subsequence(x, y, z):     m = len(x)     n = len(y)     p = len(z)          L = [[[0 for i in range(p+1)] for j in range(n+1)] for k in range(m+1)]          for i in range(m+1):         for j in range(n+1):             for k in range(p+1):                 if (i == 0 or j == 0 or k == 0):                     L[i][j][k] = 0                 elif (x[i-1] == y[j-1] and x[i-1] == z[k-1]):                     L[i][j][k] = L[i-1][j-1][k-1] + 1                 else:                     L[i][j][k] = max(max(L[i-1][j][k], L[i][j-1][k]), L[i][j][k-1])          return L[m][n][p] ```  For additional details, consult https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/lcs-longest-common-subsequence-three-strings/.

Django is a high-level Python web framework that encourages rapid development and clean, pragmatic design. React is a JavaScript library for building user interfaces. It is maintained by Facebook and a community of individual developers and companies.  For real-time communication, we will use WebRTC, an open-source project that provides web browsers and mobile applications with real-time communication via simple APIs. It allows audio and video communication to work inside web pages by allowing direct peer-to-peer communication.  For the chat feature, we will use Django Channels, which extends Django to handle WebSockets, chat protocols, IoT protocols, and more. It's built on a Python specification called ASGI.  For the recording feature, we will use MediaRecorder API, which is used to record audio and video, and can be used together with the streams API.  ## Python package name ```python "video_conferencing_tool" ```  ## self.room_group_name = 'chat_%s' % self.room_name          # Join room group         await self.channel_layer.group_add(             self.room_group_name,             self.channel_name         )          await self.accept()      async def disconnect(self, close_code):         # Leave room group         await self.channel_layer.group_discard(             self.room_group_name,             self.channel_name         )      # Receive message from WebSocket     async def receive(self, text_data):         text_data_json = json.loads(text_data)         message = text_data_json['message']          # Send message to room group         await self.channel_layer.group_send(             self.room_group_name,             {                 'type': 'chat_message',                 'message': message             }         )      # Receive message from room group     async def chat_message(self, event):         message = event['message']          # Send message to WebSocket         await &lt;p&gt; Consider a fair coin that is continuously flipped until two successive heads are achieved. Assume these are achieved on the $(M-1)$th and $M$th flip.&lt;br&gt; Let's define $P(n)$ as the likelihood that $M$ is a multiple of $n$. For instance, the sequences HH, HTHH, and THTTHH all contribute to $P(2)$, but THH and HTTHH do not.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is given that $P(2) =\frac 3 5$ and $P(3)=\frac 9  {31}$. It can be demonstrated that $P(n)$ is invariably a rational number.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For a prime number $p$ and a completely simplified fraction $\frac a b$, let's define $Q(\frac a b,p)$ as the smallest positive $q$ such that $a \equiv b q \pmod{p}$.&lt;br&gt; For instance, $Q(P(2), 109) = Q(\frac 3 5, 109) = 66$, since $5 \cdot 66 = 330 \equiv 3 \pmod{109}$ and $66$ is the smallest positive number satisfying this condition.&lt;br&gt; In a similar manner, $Q(P(3),109) = 46$.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Determine the value of $Q(P(10^{18}),1\,000\,000\,009)$.&lt;/p&gt;

It takes a list of strings, `words`, a string `separator` as inputs, and two optional boolean parameters: `odd_positions_only` (default to False) and `consecutive_primes` (default to False). If `odd_positions_only` is True, the `separator` should only be inserted between consecutive elements with odd indexes. If `consecutive_primes` is True, the `separator` should only be inserted between consecutive elements with prime indexes.  Example usage: - concatenate_elements([], "-") returns "" - concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-") returns "one-two-three" - concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-", odd_positions_only=True) returns "one-two-three" - concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-", consecutive_primes=True) returns "one-two-three"  Code to be completed: from typing import List  def concatenate_elements(words: List[str], separator: str, odd_positions_only: bool = False, consecutive_primes: bool = False) -> str:     """ Combine the elements of the input list 'words' using the string 'separator'     >>> concatenate_elements([], "-")     ""     >>> concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-")     "one-two-three"     >>> concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-", odd_positions_only=True)     "one-two-three"     >>> concatenate_elements(["one", "two", "three"], "-", consecutive_primes=True)     "one-two-three"     """

Craft a fragment of code deploying the database manipulation language, Structured Query Language (SQL), aimed at obliging the systematic extraction of the complete assortment of attributes from a database table identified under the moniker "Users". can you rewrite this function in perl? import tensorflow as tf from tensorflow.keras import layers import numpy as np from tensorflow.keras.datasets import cifar10  BUFFER_SIZE = 60000 BATCH_SIZE = 256  # Load CIFAR-10 dataset (train_images, train_labels), (_, _) = cifar10.load_data()  TRAIN_BUF=60000  # Batch and shuffle the data train_dataset = tf.data.Dataset.from_tensor_slices((train_images, train_labels)).shuffle(TRAIN_BUF).batch(BATCH_SIZE)  # Create the generator def generator_model():     model = tf.keras.Sequential()     model.add(layers.Dense(8*8*256, use_bias=False, input_shape=(100,)))     model.add(layers.BatchNormalization())     model.add(layers.LeakyReLU())      model.add(layers.Reshape((8, 8, 256)))      model.add(layers.Conv2DTranspose(128, (5, 5), strides=(1, 1), padding='same', use_bias=False))     model.add(layers.BatchNormalization())     model.add(layers.LeakyReLU())      model.add(layers.Conv2DTranspose(64, (5, 5), strides=(2, 2), padding='same', use_bias=False))     model.add(layers.BatchNormalization())     model.add(layers.LeakyReLU())      model.add(layers.Conv2DTranspose(3, (5, 5), strides=(2, 2), padding='same', use_bias=False, activation='tanh'))      return model  # Create the discriminator def discriminator_model():     model = tf.keras.Sequential()     model.add(layers.Conv2D(64, (5, 5), strides=(2, 2), padding='same', input_shape=[32, 32, 3]))     model.add(layers.LeakyReLU())     model.add(layers.Dropout(0.3))      model.add(layers.Conv2D(128, (5, 5), strides=(2, 2), padding='same'))     model.add(layers.LeakyReLU())     model.add(layers.Dropout(0.3))      model.add(layers.Flatten())     model.add(layers.Dense(1))      return model  generator = generator_model() discriminator = discriminator_model()  # Define the loss and optimizer cross_entropy = tf.keras.losses.BinaryCrossentropy(from_logits=True)  def generator_loss(generated_output):     return cross_entropy(tf.ones_like(generated_output), generated_output)  def discriminator_loss(real_output, generated_output):     real_loss = cross_entropy(tf.ones_like(real_output), real_output)     generated_loss = cross_entropy(tf.zeros_like(generated_output), generated_output)     total_loss = real_loss + generated_loss      return total_loss  generator_optimizer = tf.keras.optimizers.Adam(1e-4) discriminator_optimizer = tf.keras.optimizers.Adam(1e-4)  @tf.function def train_step(images):     noise = tf.random.normal([BATCH_SIZE, 100])      with tf.GradientTape() as gen_tape, tf.GradientTape() as disc_tape:         generated_images = generator(noise, training=True)            real_output = discriminator(images, training=True)         generated_output = discriminator(generated_images, training=True)          gen_loss = generator_loss(generated_output)         disc_loss = discriminator_loss(real_output, generated_output)      gradients_of_gen = gen_tape.gradient(gen_loss, generator.trainable_variables)     gradients_of_disc = disc_tape.gradient(disc_loss, discriminator.trainable_variables)      generator_optimizer.apply_gradients(zip(gradients_of_gen, generator.trainable_variables))     discriminator_optimizer.apply_gradients(zip(gradients_of_disc, discriminator.trainable_variables))  # Train the GAN EPOCHS = 50 for epoch in range(EPOCHS):     for image_batch in train_dataset:         train_step(image_batch) it would be great if you could include comments to clarify the perl code.
Write a multi-threaded C++ code to generate a sequence of Fibonacci series up to nth place. The application should divide the computational work between several threads and manage this concurrently to improve the efficiency. Write me the Rust version of the following code with the same logic: def complex_prime_sort(l1: list, l2: list):     """     Gather a sorted list of even numbers which are simultaneously prime from two listed sets.     """      def merge_sort(m: list, n: list):         # Combine and sort the lists in reverse order         merged = m + n         merged.sort(reverse=True)         return merged      def prime_check(x: int):         # Confirm the primality of the integer, while note that 2 is the only even prime number         if x < 2:             return False         for i in range(2, int(x**0.5) + 1):             if x % i == 0:                 return False         return True      list_prime_even = []      for num1 in l1 + l2:  # Combining two lists and iterating over         if num1 % 2 == 0 and prime_check(num1):  # Check if number is even and prime             list_prime_even.append(num1)      return merge_sort(list_prime_even, [])  # Execute merge_sort function with the list of even primes  print(complex_prime_sort([-1, 2, -4, 5, 6], [5, 3, -5, 2, -3, 3, 8, 0, 14, 4, -10]))  # [2,2]

Rewrite this in Javascript: import java.util.List; import java.util.Arrays;  public class Solution {     public List<String> charMinList(List<String> lst1, List<String> lst2) {         int charCount1 = 0;         int charCount2 = 0;          for(String s : lst1) {             charCount1 += s.length();         }          for(String s: lst2){             charCount2 += s.length();         }          return charCount1 <= charCount2 ? lst1 : lst2;     }          public static void main(String[] args) {         Solution solution = new Solution();         System.out.println(solution.charMinList(Arrays.asList("example", "test"), Arrays.asList("exam", "trial"))); // should return ["exam", "trial"]         System.out.println(solution.charMinList(Arrays.asList("demo", "project"), Arrays.asList("demo", "project", "requirements"))); // should return ["demo", "project"]         System.out.println(solution.charMinList(Arrays.asList("4"), Arrays.asList("1", "2", "3", "4", "5"))); // should return ["4"]     } }

I am faced with this challenge: Maximum Lexicographical Concatenation of Two Strings with a Twist. The details are as follows: You are provided with two strings, denoted as `word1` and `word2`. Your task is to create a string, referred to as `merge`, using the following guidelines: as long as either `word1` or `word2` are not empty, select one of the following alternatives:  If `word1` is not empty, append the initial character in `word1` to `merge` and remove it from `word1`.  For instance, if `word1 = "abc"` and `merge = "dv"`, then after selecting this operation, `word1 = "bc"` and `merge = "dva"`.  If `word2` is not empty, append the initial character in `word2` to `merge` and remove it from `word2`.  For instance, if `word2 = "abc"` and `merge = ""`, then after selecting this operation, `word2 = "bc"` and `merge = "a"`.  Your goal is to return the lexicographically maximum `merge` you can create. However, there is a twist. You are also given a third string `word3` which is a sequence of '1's and '2's. The sequence determines from which word you should take the initial character. If the character in `word3` is '1', take the initial character from `word1`, if it is '2', take the initial character from `word2`. If the word from which you are supposed to take the character is empty, take the character from the other word.  A string `a` is considered lexicographically larger than a string `b` (of the same length) if at the first position where `a` and `b` differ, `a` has a character strictly larger than the corresponding character in `b`. For instance, `"abcd"` is lexicographically larger than `"abcc"` because the first position they differ is at the fourth character, and `d` is greater than `c`.  Example 1: Input: word1 = "cabaa", word2 = "bcaaa", word3 = "12121" Output: "cbacabaaaa";  Example 2: Input: word1 = "abcabc", word2 = "abdcaba", word3 = "2121212" Output: "abdcabcabcaba";  Constraints: `1 <= word1.length, word2.length, word3.length <= 3000` `word1` and `word2` consist solely of lowercase English alphabets. `word3` consists solely of '1's and '2's.

could you refactor this code to php? import math import sympy  def is_integer(n):     eps = 1.0e-12  # floating point precision     return abs(n - round(n)) < eps  def accurate_largest_prime_factor(n: float):     """Return the largest prime factor of a positive, negative number of a decimal figure,     on the condition that abs(n) > 1 and is not a prime number itself."""     if n == 0:         return None     if n < 0:         n = -n  # take the absolute value     if is_integer(n) and sympy.isprime(int(n)):         return None  # n itself is prime     max_prime = 2.0     while n % 2 == 0:  # remove any factor of 2         n = n / 2         max_prime = 2.0     for i in range(3, int(math.sqrt(n))+1, 2):          while n % i == 0:              n = n / i             max_prime = i     if n > 2:          max_prime = n  # n itself is prime     if is_integer(max_prime):         max_prime = int(max_prime)  # return an integer if possible     return max_prime would be helpful to have comments explaining the php code.

Consider a sequence that begins with the integers $2, 3, \dots, n$. In each iteration, every integer in the sequence is divided by its smallest prime factor, and the product of these smallest prime factors is appended to the sequence as a new integer. Any integers that have been reduced to $1$ are subsequently eliminated from the sequence. For instance, the first three iterations for $n = 5$ would proceed as follows: $$[2, 3, 4, 5] \xrightarrow{(1)} [2, 60] \xrightarrow{(2)} [30, 4] \xrightarrow{(3)} [15, 2, 4].$$ Let's denote $S(n, m)$ as the sum of all integers in the sequence after $m$ iterations. For instance, $S(5, 3) = 15 + 2 + 4 = 21$ and $S(10, 100) = 257$. Your task is to calculate $S(10^4, 10^{16})$, with the result given modulo $1234567891$.

I am facing a challenge with the following problem titled: Advanced Chalkboard Addition Game. The problem is described as follows: We have non-negative integers, denoted as nums[i], inscribed on a chalkboard. Two players, Alice and Bob, alternate in erasing exactly one number from the chalkboard, with Alice initiating the game. If the action of erasing a number results in the sum of all the chalkboard elements equating to 0, the player performing the action loses. Conversely, if a player commences their turn with the sum of all chalkboard elements being 0, they win.  In this enhanced version of the game, Alice and Bob have the additional option to replace a chalkboard number with its half, rounded down to the nearest integer. This action is only permissible if the number is even and greater than 0.  The task is to determine if Alice can win the game, given that both players employ optimal strategies. Return True if Alice emerges as the winner.  For instance, consider the input: nums = [1, 1, 2]. The output is false, as Alice has three options: erase 1, erase 2, or replace 2 with 1. In all scenarios, Alice ends up losing the game.  Please note the constraints: `1 &lt;= N &lt;= 1000` and `0 &lt;= nums[i] &lt;= 2^16`.  The following Python code, which attempts to solve the problem, is flawed: ```python def canAliceWin(nums):     if sum(nums) == 0:         return True     for i in range(len(nums)):         temp = nums.pop(i)         if sum(nums) != 0:             nums.insert(i, temp)         else:             return False     return True ``` The code erroneously presumes that Alice will invariably win if the sum of the numbers is not 0 following her turn, which is not always true.

i'm curious about the lua translation of this code: import psycopg2 from psycopg2.extras import RealDictCursor from psycopg2 import sql, extensions  def execute_redshift_query(clusters, query):     results = []          for cluster in clusters:         conn = None         curr = None         try:             # Connect to the database             conn = psycopg2.connect(dbname=cluster['dbname'], host=cluster['host'],                                     port=cluster['port'], user=cluster['user'],                                     password=cluster['password'])              # Create a new cursor             curr = conn.cursor(cursor_factory=RealDictCursor)             conn.set_isolation_level(extensions.ISOLATION_LEVEL_AUTOCOMMIT)                          # Execute the query             curr.execute(query)              # Fetch the results             rows = curr.fetchall()             curr_summary = {'cluster': cluster['host'], 'result': rows}             results.append(curr_summary)          except Exception as e:             # Print the error             print(f"Error executing the query on {cluster['host']}: {e}")         finally:             # Close the cursor and the connection             if curr:                 curr.close()             if conn:                 conn.close()     return results

transform this code to swift for me: from collections import Counter  def get_evens_primes_sum_repeats(l1: list, l2: list):     """Return even, prime numbers from both lists, merged, sorted in descending order and sum of repeats.     Implement helper functions to detect prime numbers and sum repeats.     """          def is_prime(n):         # Helper function to check if a number is prime         if n == 2:             return True         if n < 2 or n % 2 == 0:             return False         for current in range(3, int(n ** 0.5) + 1, 2):             if n % current == 0:                  return False         return True      def merge_sort_sum(m: list, n: list):          # Helper function for merging, sorting lists and sum repeats         total_list = m + n         repeated_numbers = [item for item, count in Counter(total_list).items() if count > 1]         return sorted([num for num in set(total_list) if num > 0 and num % 2 == 0 and is_prime(num)], reverse=True), sum(repeated_numbers)      return merge_sort_sum(l1, l2)  print(get_evens_primes_sum_repeats([-1, 2, -4, 5, 6], [5, 3, -5, 2, -3, 3, 8, 0, 14, 4, -10])) i'd like to fully understand the translation; could you add notes to the swift code?

If the coffee shop owner is moody on the i-th minute, `moody[i] = 1`, otherwise `moody[i] = 0`. When the coffee shop owner is moody, the customers of that minute are not satisfied, otherwise they are satisfied.  The coffee shop owner knows a secret technique to keep themselves not moody for `X` minutes straight, but can only use it once.  Return the maximum number of customers that can be satisfied throughout the day.  Example 1: Input: customers = [2,0,1,3,1,1,8,6], moody = [0,1,0,1,0,1,0,1], X = 4 Output: 21 Explanation: The coffee shop owner keeps themselves not moody for the last 4 minutes.  The maximum number of customers that can be satisfied = 2 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 8 + 6 = 21. """      def post(self, request):         """         Handle POST requests.         """         data = request.POST         username = data.get('username')         password = data.get('password')         email = data.get('email')         interests = data.get('interests')          if not all([username, password, email, interests]):             return JsonResponse({'error': 'Missing required information.'}, status=400)          user = User.objects.create_user(username=username, password=password, email=email, interests=interests)         user.save()          return JsonResponse({'message': 'User created successfully.'}, status=201)      def get(self, request):         """         Handle GET requests.         """         username = request.GET.get('username')         password = request.GET.get('password')          if not all([username, password]):             return JsonResponse({'error': 'Missing required information.'}, status=400)          user = authenticate(request, username=username, password=password)          if user is not None:             login(request, user)             return JsonResponse({'message': 'Login successful.'}, status=200)         else:             return JsonResponse({'error': 'Invalid login credentials.'}, status=401)      def delete(self, request):         """         Handle DELETE requests.         """         logout(request)         return JsonResponse({'message': 'Logout successful.'}, status=200) , Engineer: from django.urls import path from .views import UserView  urlpatterns = [     path('', UserView.as_view(), name='user'), ] , Engineer: from django.db import models from users.models import User  class Content(models.Model):     """     Content model that represents the content created by users. You need to debug and resolve any errors, before building upon it:  ```java public class Node {     public Node[] children;     public boolean isWordEnd;     public Node() {         this.isWordEnd = false;         this.children = new Node[26];         for(int idx=0; idx<26; idx++) {             children[idx] = null;         }     } }  public class Trie {     Node root;     public Trie() {         root = new Node()     }      public void insert(String key) {         Node curr = root;         for(int i=0; i<key.length(); i++) {             if(curr.children[key.charAt(i) - 'a'] == null)                 curr.children[key.charAt(i) - 'a'] = new Node();             else                 curr = curr.children[key.charAt(i) - 'a'];         }         curr.isWordEnd = true;       }      public boolean search(String key) {         Node curr = root;         for(int i=0; i<key.length(); i++) {             if(curr.children[key.charAt(i) - 'a'] == null)                 return false;             else                 curr = curr.children[key.charAt(i) - 'a'];         }         return curr.isWordEnd;     } } ```   Apart from basic functionalities, your solution should also efficiently manage the memory if strings were deleted from the Trie. The problem is defined as follows: You are provided with the `root` of a binary tree, where each node carries a value of either `0` or `1`. Each path from the root to a leaf node signifies a binary number, with the most significant bit at the start. For instance, a path `0 -> 1 -> 1 -> 0 -> 1` could be interpreted as `01101` in binary, equivalent to `13`.  The task is to consider all the leaf nodes in the tree and calculate the sum of the numbers represented by the path from the root to each leaf. The sum of these numbers, which is guaranteed to fit within a 32-bits integer, should be returned.   Additionally, you are required to calculate the sum of the binary numbers represented by the path from the root to each node (not just the leaf nodes). The sum of these numbers, which is guaranteed to fit within a 64-bits integer, should also be returned.  For instance, consider the following examples: Example 1: Input: root = [1,0,1,0,1,0,1] Output: 22, 42 Explanation: (100) + (101) + (110) + (111) = 4 + 5 + 6 + 7 = 22 for leaf nodes. Including all nodes, (1) + (10) + (100) + (101) + (110) + (111) = 1 + 2 + 4 + 5 + 6 + 7 = 42  Example 2: Input: root = [0] Output: 0, 0  Example 3: Input: root = [1] Output: 1, 1  Example 4: Input: root = [1,1] Output: 3, 4 Explanation: (11) = 3 for leaf nodes. Including all nodes, (1) + (11) = 1 + 3 = 4  The constraints for this problem are as follows: The tree will contain nodes in the range `[1, 1000]`.  The value of `Node.val` will be either `0` or `1`.

can this be written in rust? import math  def data_process(lst, index=0, prod=1, sum_set=set()):     if index >= len(lst):   # end of list, return product and sum of distinct elements' square root         sum_r = sum([math.sqrt(v) if v%2 == 0 else 0 for v in sum_set])         return prod, round(sum_r, 2)     else:         if lst[index]%2 != 0:  # if odd             prod *= lst[index] # multiply with product         if lst[index]%2 == 0:  # if even             sum_set.add(int(lst[index]))  # add to set of distinct evens         return data_process(lst, index+2, prod, sum_set)  # continue with next index  print(data_process([3, 5.5, 7, 8, 9.3, 4, 4])) please include comments in the translated code to help me understand it.
Could you translate this snippet to PHP? def how_many_times(string: str, substring: str) -> int:     """ Appraise the count of a specified character subsequence present in the input character sequence, considering interlocked occurrences. """     return sum(1 for i in range(len(string)) if string.startswith(substring, i))  def count_subsequences(string: str, substring: str) -> int:     """ Determine the occurrences of a designated character subsequence unveiling as a discrete sequence within the input character sequence. """     counts = start = 0     while start < len(string):         pos = string.find(substring, start)         if pos != -1:             start = pos + len(substring)             counts += 1         else:             break     return counts

Args:     phrase1 (str): The first phrase.     phrase2 (str): The second phrase.     special_symbols (List[str]): List of special symbols to be considered.          Returns:     bool: True if both phrases share an identical set of unique terms, False otherwise.     """     # Define a function which removes leading/trailing punctuation and special symbols from a word     def clean_word(word: str) -> str:         while word and word[0] in string.punctuation + ''.join(special_symbols):             word = word[1:]         while word and word[-1] in string.punctuation + ''.join(special_symbols):             word = word[:-1]         return word          # Split phrases into words, remove punctuation and special symbols,     # and convert words to lowercase (to ignore case sensitivity)     words1 = set(clean_word(word) for word in phrase1.lower().split())     words2 = set(clean_word(word) for word in phrase2.lower().split())          # Compare sets of words     return words1 == words2

Additionally, the method should handle large-sized strings efficiently.   For example, given the strings: str_1 = "Listen!" str_2 = "Silent"  The function should return TRUE.

Could you please make this work in C++? import boto3 import logging from botocore.exceptions import BotoCoreError, ClientError  class AWSInterface():     def __init__(self, s3_bucket_name, rds_endpoint):         # Initialize boto3 client for S3 and RDS         self.s3 = boto3.client('s3')         self.rds = boto3.client('rds')         self.s3_bucket_name = s3_bucket_name         self.rds_endpoint = rds_endpoint         # Initialize logger         self.logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)         self.logger.setLevel(logging.INFO)      def execute_query(self, query):         try:             result = self.rds.execute_statement(                 resourceArn = self.rds_endpoint,                 secretArn = '<secret_arn>',                 database = '<database>',                 sql = query             )         except ClientError as e:             self.logger.error(f"Failed to execute query: {e}")             return None         except BotoCoreError as e:             self.logger.error(f"BotoCoreError: {e}")             return None         return result      def upload_to_s3(self, file_path, object_name=None):         if not object_name:             object_name = file_path         try:             self.s3.upload_file(file_path, self.s3_bucket_name, object_name)         except ClientError as e:             self.logger.error(f"Failed to upload file to S3: {e}")             return False         except BotoCoreError as e:             self.logger.error(f"BotoCoreError: {e}")             return False         return True      def download_from_s3(self, object_name, file_path=None):         if not file_path:             file_path = object_name         try:             self.s3.download_file(self.s3_bucket_name, object_name, file_path)         except ClientError as e:             self.logger.error(f"Failed to download file from S3: {e}")             return False         except BotoCoreError as e:             self.logger.error(f"BotoCoreError: {e}")             return False         return True
